# Global register of open findings

One row per open finding, keyed by the requirement or artifact id it concerns. Each row
names the work item that carried it, the phase, and the decision.

A finding is not removed when it resolves. Its row is annotated in place — **Resolved
<date>**, naming the PR or commit that discharged it and quoting what the annotation
supersedes — because a deleted row leaves every citation to it dangling, and
`scripts/audit-docs.py` check 25 resolves finding citations against this file. A row is
removed only when the phase whose register carries it is archived.

An unowned row must name the event that next confirms or assigns its owner. Absent a named
event it decays to the next `CLAUDE.md` §14 plan review, which must give it a disposition
rather than merely list it; a row that reaches a review and leaves it unchanged is a review
finding, not a register one.

A Decision cell opens with one of: the register's own disposition vocabulary — `fix before
close`, `accept`, `carry forward`, `split verdict` — each with its qualifiers (`with an
owner`, `with a trigger`, `unowned`, `phase boundary`, `provisional owner`); or `CLAUDE.md`
§13's four verdicts — `delivered but untested`, `deferred with an owner`, `reassigned`,
`not started` — which are binding, already appear (F53, F54), and may not be linted away by
a register-local vocabulary; or the negated form F37 and F40 need — a decision that a fix is
**not available** or **not required** before close, which must state where the work goes
instead. Every row carries one of the five ownership shapes: workstream, event, trigger,
next-toucher, unowned-pending-authorisation.

Status is not a Decision. The five rows that write one there (F-W10-1-1, F-W10-2-1,
F-W10-2-2, F32, F28) are corrected when P4 gives status its own field, or sooner.

**Every data row begins with a literal `|`.** GFM itself does not require this — a table
row missing its leading pipe still renders — but `scripts/register-lint.py`'s parser treats
that leading `|` as the sole test for whether a line is even a candidate row: a line
without it is skipped (`if not line.startswith("|"): continue`) before it is counted, before
its fields are split, and before the `assert classified == seen` accounting guard that
exists specifically to make a silent row-loss loud can see it — that guard cannot detect a
class of loss its own counted population never included. The table-contiguity check added
after F52 (`a3b9c9e`) does report such a row as a structural problem, because it still
carries four or more unescaped pipes and so falls inside the swept range — the gap it
leaves is loud — but the row itself is still not parsed: no `Row` is produced, and none of
its five fields is read. A row loses its leading `|` only by mistake; nothing but this
sentence keeps one from being written that way.

| Error codes across the spec/code boundary (F29) | Nothing checks error codes in either direction. `scripts/audit-docs.py`'s check 10 reads only `docs/specs/*.md` and tests only that no code is claimed as owned by two modules; it never opens `backend/src/app/errors.py`. | NFR-500 names no storage format (F37) | The requirement budgets trace storage at 1 % of 50 M annual quotes under 200 GB/year but names no storage format, so it is silent on the variable that decides it. Measured at the 200-step structure NFR-489 itself names: one persisted `Trace` is 1,032,135 B uncompressed, which is 2.6x the 400 kB per trace that 200 GB over 500,000 traces allows, and 14,523 B under gzip -6, which is 4 % of budget at 71.1x compression. The redundancy that makes tracing slow is exactly what makes it compress — each of 189 steps carries a near-identical copy of the accumulated context. Two caveats make the uncompressed case worse: FR-259 persists 1 % plus 100 % of declines and errors, so the annual count exceeds 500,000, and the 50 M is a quote count rather than step-count weighted. Trace size is super-linear in step count (113 kB at 63 steps, 1.03 MB at 200). | NFR-489's without-GBM half not established (F38) | `docs/research/w11-task-1-5-nfr-rate-1-2.md` recorded "Both halves PASS" with the without-GBM half at p99 9.410 ms against a 15 ms bound. Five runs of the committed harness give 9.410 PASS, 10.162 PASS, 14.076 PASS at 94 % of budget, 24.816 OVER and 26.725 OVER — two of five breach. The correct verdict is measured, verdict unstable across runs, not established. Load does not order the results (the quietest and the busiest both passed) but it widens the spread about fivefold: stdev 3.5-4.0 ms under load against 0.706 ms quiet, so a quiet run's narrow spread makes one run look more trustworthy than it is. The leaf plan required the distribution reported, and the harness printed stdev, p50 and p90 for every run while the note dropped them — but reporting them WOULD NOT have revealed this, and run 1's own recorded figures show why. Its mean is 6.915 ms and its max 12.959 ms, so its largest sample sits 6.044 ms above the mean; at the 3.5-4.0 ms stdev the audit measured on its own re-runs that is only 1.5 to 1.7 sigma, where the expected maximum of 1,000 samples is about 3.24 sigma for a normal distribution (simulated over 20,000 trials: mean 3.243, median 3.198; the 3.7 first written here was sqrt(2 ln 1000) = 3.717, which is the leading asymptotic term rather than the expectation, and about 15 % high) and further out for a right-skewed latency one. Run 1's spread was therefore necessarily narrow, near run 5's 0.706 ms, and printed it would have read as reassuring. So the criterion as written is satisfiable by a single quiet run that still gets the verdict wrong. WHAT ESTABLISHES THIS VERDICT IS REPETITION UNDER VARIED LOAD; the distribution explains the result once you have both, rather than revealing it. The acceptance criterion needs both and carried only one. The with-GBM half is PASS in 5 of 5 but its margin narrows from about 4.0x to about 1.8x. Found by the PR #416 audit finding 1 | W11-1.5 | 2 | carry forward with an owner — NFR-489's full-path and sustained-load halves are Slice 2 Task 2D's, which must re-measure the component half with the distribution and enough runs to establish a verdict. | Test-database cleanup is teardown-only, so any interrupted run poisons the next (F40) | `backend/tests/conftest_db.py`'s `_empty_the_database_after_the_session` truncates the shared Postgres only AFTER a session ends, and there is no setup-side guard. The session scope is deliberate — its docstring records that truncating per test would cost a full-table lock 430 times over — but it assumes every run reaches teardown. Any run that does not (a killed gate, Ctrl-C, a crash, an OOM) leaves its rows behind, and the next run on that box fails. The damage is misattributed by construction: it surfaces as `UniqueViolationError` on `uq_users_issuer_subject` inside `backend/tests/test_api_datasets.py`, a file unrelated to whatever the interrupted run was doing, so nothing points the next person at the real cause. The box is shared across worktrees, so the victim is usually someone else. A second, smaller weakness compounds it: two tests INSERT hardcoded identity literals — `(https://provider.example, analyst)` and the same issuer with `owner` — with no randomisation, so they collide with any surviving row rather than generating a fresh key. Observed 2026-08-30 during PR #416's certifying gate: 2 failed / 2292 passed, reconciling against `--collect-only`'s 2297. Root cause was three gate runs killed mid-pytest, which the lead caused by merging register PRs while a gate was in flight. **The tests then passed in isolation** — because the completing run's own teardown had by then emptied the database — which is a misleading green the diagnosis nearly stopped at; `users` was verified at 0 rows directly against Postgres rather than inferred. Found by w11-executor-416fix | W11-1.5 | 2 | fix before close is not required — no shipped behaviour is affected. Empty the database at session START as well as teardown, so a poisoned box self-heals rather than failing a later unrelated run; and randomise the two hardcoded identity literals. Owner: whoever next touches `conftest_db.py` |

Recorded here so the next agent that meets the guidance finds the ruling instead of asking. **Two corrections to this row's own measurement, both found while wiring the check.** **(1) The predicate stated above does not reproduce the figure stated above, and did not when it was written.** At `32fc63c` the bare-URL form returns **77**, not 73; the trailer form (`--grep='Claude-Session'`) returns **73**, exactly the recorded figure. The four in the difference are `2e4684b`, which merely *quotes* this predicate in its body, and `ba76b3f`, `cbb8ffb`, `d2300f0` — real bare-URL violations, but all dated **2026-08-22**, eight days before this ruling and therefore inside the population 73 was meant to count. So 73 was a **trailer** count reported under a **URL** predicate. The figure is right about the trailer and the predicate is right about nothing; `RFC-777`'s rule that a count carries the predicate it counted with is what this row fails. **(2) The instrument did leak, but by 2 and not by 4**: `b2fb122` (#530) and `fbf483e` (#527) carry the trailer and are dated 2026-08-31, after acceptance. §15's *"enforced at the merge, which is the only point where one person controls what lands"* is true about control and false about reliability, and 2 is enough to make that point without inflating it. | Service Account key issuance never mints a key for any granted environment but the first (F54) | `backend/src/app/api/service_accounts.py:180` (`create_service_account`) and `:246` (`rotate_key`) both call `generate_key(body.environments[0])` / `generate_key(account.environments[0])` — list-order first only. Neither route, nor any other in the file (`create_service_account`, `rotate_key`, `revoke_key` are the whole router — `grep -n '@router\.'`), accepts an environment parameter or otherwise offers a way to mint a key scoped to any environment other than index 0. FR-389 (`07-platform.md:73`) grants an account *"named environments"*, plural, with no maximum (`service_accounts.py:63`, `Field(min_length=1)`), so an account created e.g. `["uat", "dev"]` is schema-legal and `dev` is a real granted scope — but no API call, including `rotate_key`, can ever produce a key whose `parsed.environment` is `dev`. The auth-time check that exists for exactly this purpose, FR-430 (`07:141`, *"A `uat` key can never score against `prod`"*, enforced at `auth/service.py:210-217` — `if parsed.environment not in set(account.environments)` raises `ENVIRONMENT_SCOPE_DENIED`), can therefore only ever fire against a forged or hand-crafted key; against a legitimately issued one, `parsed.environment` is always `environments[0]` by construction, so the check's pass branch is exercised on every legitimate call and its refusal branch is exercised on none. Found and deliberately not ruled by **RL-916 §4** (`docs/rulings/RL-00916-the-field-is-the-environment-the-quote-was-served-in-the-spec-already-says-so-and-the-branch-does-not-merge-until-it-says-so-too.md`), which states it explicitly: *"it is not created, worsened or relied upon by PR #485 [...] It is reported to the lead for the findings register with an owner, and is not ruled by this record."* **Attribution: `main`**, in key issuance (`service_accounts.py`), not Task 4B (WK-671) — Task 4B only reads `caller.environment` post-authentication and neither writes nor calls `generate_key`. Source: E7 of the deputy's entry in the lead's local channel file `to-lead.md` stamped 2026-09-28 11:33:12 BST (`to-lead.md:7520`). Filed as its own row rather than folded into any Task 4B row because it is a `main` defect Task 4B neither introduced nor depends on. |
| `TraceStep.consumed`/`.produced` carry the full accumulated context, not the step's own declared inputs/outputs (F55) | `_build_trace` (`packages/pricing-core/src/pricing_core/rating/score.py:660`) builds each `TraceStep` from the zen-engine's own raw per-node trace entry — `consumed=dict(entry.get("input") or {})` and `produced=dict(output)` at `:686-687` — which carries the **full accumulated evaluation context at that node**, not only the variables the algorithm step itself declares via `consumes`/`produces`. **Verified independently against `origin/main` `1ccec01`** (this branch's base), not taken from the research note's relay: for a compiled 50-`n_expr` structure, step `s_v047` declares `consumes=['v046']` (one input) via `algorithm.steps`, but `result.trace.steps` reports that same step's `consumed` dict holding **61 keys** — every prior input and computed variable, `channel`…`v046` — and `produced` holding 62. Consumed-key count grows monotonically with step position across the whole chain (12 keys at the first computed step, 63 at the last of 50), confirming the note's "worse than linear" characterisation rather than an artifact of one probed step. **Found by WK-671 Task 4D** (`docs/research/w11-task-4d-nfr-rate-12.md` §2, PR #501) while sweeping trace size against step count for the NFR-500 projection, and it is **the single largest driver of that projection's OVER-budget verdict** (~2.58× the 200 GB/year budget at the ~200-step reference structure) — a `TraceStep` carrying only its own declared consumed/produced fields would be substantially smaller. Task 4D itself is a projection, not an implementation change, so it does not fix this; it is filed here per Task 4D's own note (§6) asking that the finding "not be read as merely an artifact of this benchmark's fixture." | WK-671 Slice 4 Task 4D | 2 | carry forward — unowned. The remedy — trimming `TraceStep.consumed`/`.produced` to each step's own declared `consumes`/`produces` rather than the engine's full per-node context, or another slimmer trace-step shape — is a design change to `pricing_core.rating.score._build_trace` and to the `Trace` schema's semantics, out of Task 4D's scope (measurement, not remediation) and named by no workstream. It is also NFR-500's largest available remediation lever if the decision-maker chooses to bring the projection back under budget by shrinking trace size, rather than lowering the sample rate or raising the budget — a choice this row does not make. ***Cross-referenced 2026-08-31*** — F35 (NFR-490's latency finding) names the identical fix as its own remedy: trimming `TraceStep.consumed`/`.produced` to each step's own declared inputs/outputs. F35's remedy was gated on RL-862's off-path trace capture, which landed in WK-671 Task 4B (`003f9d4`, #485); that clears the precondition for building this shared lever but builds neither row's fix. Nobody had connected the two rows until this cross-reference. |
| `audit-docs.py`'s notes scan root skips silently when absent (F56) | `scripts/audit-docs.py:52` defines `NOTES = REPO / ".claude" / "notes"` as one constant with two consumers: `check_notes` (`:220`, checks 16-20) guards on it at `:235-236` — `if not NOTES.is_dir(): notes.append("no docs/notes/ directory — checks 16-20 skipped"); return` — and `check_finding_citations` (`:415`, check 25) puts it in `scan_dirs` at `:505`, filtered by `if d.is_dir()`. **Absence is a skip, not a failure, in both places, and the second skip prints nothing at all.** Verified live rather than assumed: loading the script as a module and repointing `NOTES` at a non-existent path, then calling `main()` directly, returns exit code **0** and prints *"no docs/notes/ directory — checks 16-20 skipped"* followed by **"All checks passed."** — check 25 silently loses one of its three scan roots with no corresponding line anywhere in the output. **So any rename or move of `docs/notes/` that misses updating this one constant silently disables checks 16-20 and weakens check 25, with a green gate** — a hole open today for any rename, not only the one `docs/plans/PL-00929-rfc-897-file-taxonomy-reference-coding-and-custody-research-and-the-slice-cut.md` proposes. Same class as plan review 10's `fastapi-service` finding (a skill and a test both asserting a check that did not exist, its body unable to fail): a gate whose absence-case is a silent skip reports the same green as a gate that ran — `CLAUDE.md` §13's *"a check that has never printed a failure has not been tested"*, and two instances inside two days argues the class is worth naming here rather than treating each as its own coincidence. Found by the decision-maker, verified independently at source (`scripts/audit-docs.py:52,235-236,505`) and by direct reproduction before this row was filed | RFC-897 investigation | 2 | ***Resolved 2026-09-28*** (`CR-1183`, the WK-695 close; fixed by `cbf1365`, PR #540). Verified at `62d5fbae`: `scripts/audit-docs.py:461` reads `if not NOTES.is_dir():` and calls `fail(` with *"does not exist — checks 16-20 cannot run"* (`:463`); check 25's loop at `:793` fails a missing scan root the same way; `tests/test_audit_docs_scan_roots.py` 1 passed. Proposal only as of this filing — nothing scheduled, the acceptance line undated — so the owner is named, not yet discharged |

| `03` §5.2 declares `KeyFilter`'s home as `pricing_core/rate_tables/operations.py`; the code imports it from `model-schema` (F59) | `docs/specs/03-rating-engine.md:725` places `KeyFilter = dict[str, list[str]]` inside the `# pricing_core/rate_tables/operations.py` fenced block (header `03:724`), stating it as a type defined in that module. The risk is prospective rather than present: `03` §5.2 is written as an implementation reference, and a reader implementing "from the spec" rather than from the code would create a second `KeyFilter` inside `pricing_core/rate_tables/operations.py`, which is exactly the duplication `CLAUDE.md` §2 states causes a mispricing ("nobody hand-writes a shape that already exists in `model-schema`... a shape defined twice will diverge"). Filed as its own row per proposal 4.2 (plan review 10, `docs/closures/CR-00926-plan-review-10-at-wk-671-s-second-close.md:288`) rather than folded into F60, because its severity class differs: F60's four items are spec text lagging the code (an omission), this one actively misattributes a shape's canonical owner (a misdirection) with a live governance rule on point. | Plan review 10 (proposal 4.2) | 2 | **Resolved 2026-09-28** by `RL-1172` (#829, `62d5fbae`) item 2: the spec is wrong, and `03` §5.2 now shows `KeyFilter` imported from `model_schema.rating`, amended in that record's own commit. | C2's `PreToolUse` layer is bypassable exactly as C3's dissolved git hook was, and unlike C3 it has no CI-equivalent backstop (F61) | RFC-895 adoption slice G (`9e8783d`, #516) built `scripts/hooks/retry_cap_hook.py` — C2, the retry-cap hook — after RL-920 §3 dissolved its sibling C3 (the verify-gate hook) specifically because *"a git hook cannot satisfy `CLAUDE.md` §13 in principle... `--no-verify` bypasses it with no trace... no broken-input proof can establish otherwise,"* with the state it would have enforced already checked by CI, "the stronger check." C2 passes RL-920's own literal test for needing a hook at all (§2: *"a hook is needed only to intercept an action that leaves no artifact"* — a fix/replan decision is exactly that), and `record`'s own refusal is unconditional and independently mutation-proven: verified directly in this audit by mutating `_would_breach`'s `current >= cap` to `current > cap` (`scripts/hooks/retry_cap_hook.py:148`) and re-running `tests/test_retry_cap_hook.py` — 4 of 11 cases failed, matching PR #516's own reported figure exactly, then reverted. **But RL-920 §4 never asks whether C2's *registration* point is bypassable the same way C3's was, and it is**: `.claude/settings.json` is a session-scope Claude Code hook, disableable per-session (`disableAllHooks`) or overridden by the gitignored `.claude/settings.local.json` (`.gitignore:82`) exactly as C3's git hook was uninstalled-by-clone and `--no-verify`-bypassable — the two escape hatches PR #516's own body names in the same sentence it uses to distinguish C2 from C3 ("nothing stops an actor from hand-editing `retry_counters`... disabling hooks for a session... or simply never calling `record`"). **The asymmetry is the finding**: C3 was disqualified for being a bypassable local mechanism *with* CI sitting behind it as a stronger, unbypassable backstop; C2 is a bypassable local mechanism *with no backstop at all* — nothing in this repository re-derives `retry_counters` from durable artifacts (`delivery-process.core.json:249`'s own design note: *"Neither stops a direct hand-edit of the runtime state file or a session with hooks disabled -- no CI-equivalent backstop exists behind C2 (RL-920 §4)"*; RL-907(b) assigns that reconciliation to "a future watcher cycle," not built anywhere yet — consistent with F58, which independently finds no live watcher-cycle writer of any kind exists today). RL-920 §4 asserts hooks are "the only registration point that reaches" the acting agent without weighing this against §3's own bypass reasoning three paragraphs earlier in the same ruling — the two sections were not reconciled against each other. **This is not closed by the executor having disclosed the gap in the PR body and the process-core note**: honesty about a limitation is not the limitation's remedy, and C2 has now shipped and is in force | RFC-895 adoption slice G | 2 | **accept** — the residual gap (C2's layer is bypassable while the retry counters are absent) is ACCEPTED in writing, the row's own option (b), 2026-09-27 (`CR-1167`, plan review 14; accepted by delegation 2026-09-27 16:18:24 BST). Owner: the lead. Event: plan review 15, where it is re-read against artifact B's counters if they exist by then. The writer's docstring (`write_runtime_state.py:22-24`) says the hook does not exist, which is stale: `scripts/hooks/retry_cap_hook.py:135` reads `retry_counters`. This supersedes the owner and event of the opening kept below: carry forward with an owner — no slice or workstream today builds the re-derivation cycle (RL-907(b)) that would give C2 a CI-equivalent backstop, and none is scheduled. Two dispositions are open, not one: (a) build the reconciliation — a mechanism that periodically or on-demand recomputes `retry_counters` from a durable, harder-to-forge source (e.g. the `record`/`hook` invocation's own audit trail, if one is added) and flags a mismatch; or (b) the lead or maintainer explicitly accepts the residual gap as proportionate, in writing, rather than by silence. Needs the lead to name which, and by when — absent a named event this decays to the next `CLAUDE.md` §14 phase review per this register's own header rule |

| `register-lint.py` (check 29) was blind to 11 of 59 register rows and printed `OK (0 violations)` throughout (F64) | `scripts/register-lint.py`'s `parse_register`, as it shipped in `fa87086` (#519, check 29's own introduction) and stood through `890b06e` (#520, F62's own filing), reset `in_table = False` on every line not starting with `&#124;` — including the two blank lines the register's one data table carries for readability, after F51's row and after F52's row. Once reset by the first of those, the parser only re-enters table mode on a separator row (`&#124;---&#124;---&#124;...`) or the table's own header row (naming its five column headings) — neither reappears — so every row from F52 onward was silently skipped: not read, not counted, not linted. **Verified directly against the pre-fix code**: running that `parse_register` (`git show 890b06e:scripts/register-lint.py`) over the register as it stood at `890b06e` returns **48 rows, not 59** — 11 short, exactly F52, F53, F54, F55, F56, F57, F58, F59, F60, F61, F62. `main()` printed `"OK (0 violations)"` at every one of those commits regardless, because zero violations were found among the rows it did see, with no signal anywhere that 11 rows (19% of the register at that point, including every row filed in the two days before this one) went unexamined. **This directly undercuts an assurance given in this session**: told "`register-lint.py` returns 0 violations, so your F62 row passes check 29," that run had not examined F62's row at all — true only by coincidence, not by verification. Fixed in `f99b55d` (#521, alongside `scripts/register-owed.py`) by removing the blank-line reset, with a regression test; verified independently against the current tree: `parse_register` now returns 59 rows, F62 included, 0 violations — confirming both the fix and that F62's row was genuinely clean, just not for the reason originally stated. Filed for the record: a check merged, audited, and reported clean by two different people across two different commits was blind to a fifth of its own target corpus the entire time it ran. | RFC-896 P5 | 2 | accept — the defect is fixed and regression-tested (`f99b55d`, #521); no owner is owed further work. The event that would reopen this row is a future regression in `parse_register`'s blank-line handling — guarded today by `tests/test_register_lint.py`'s new coverage, not by this row |

| `write_runtime_state.py cycle` cannot clear a field — "no active slice" is inexpressible (F69) | `_position_block_content` starts from a copy of the existing position content and overwrites only `phase`/`work`/`slice` when the matching CLI flag is not `None`; `--slice` defaults to `None` and has no separate clear flag, so omitting it silently carries the previous value forward. Empirically reproduced against the shipped script (89dd2b1): a state file seeded with `slice.value = "old-value"`, then `cycle --work WK-695` omitting `--slice`, leaves `slice.value` at `old-value`, byte-identical, while `work` updates correctly. This defeats the artifact's own stated design — position re-derived every cycle so a dead or unwired writer cannot masquerade as a healthy zero — for exactly this field. A second, separate claim relayed alongside this one (that the same script re-stamps `written_at` on every cycle regardless of content change) was retracted by the lead before filing and independently re-checked here: two identical `cycle` calls seconds apart leave `written_at` unmoved on both blocks, confirming the retraction; it is not part of this finding. | `written_at` records when a block's content last changed, not when the writer last checked it (F72) | Live instance, read directly from `~/gi-pricing-plan.local/handover/runtime-state.json` at filing time: `position.written_at` current to the minute (`2026-09-02T02:02:13Z`) while `in_flight_expensive_verifications.written_at` sits at `2026-09-01T20:06:20Z` with `entries: []` — six hours old, truthful (nothing changed since), and useless for the question a reader actually asks: is this being maintained right now, or is it a dead writer's residue. `write_runtime_state.py cycle` carries the whole old block, stamps included, forward unchanged whenever freshly re-derived content matches what's on disk, writing a new `written_at` only on an actual content change — re-proven empirically for this filing, as for F69: two identical `cycle` calls leave `written_at` unmoved on both blocks. **This is not a defect**: it is `docs/rulings/RL-00907-q4-artifacts-win-where-an-artifact-exists-and-nothing-that-blocks-an-action-may-be-counted-in-b-without-one.md` **RL-907(d)'s own acceptance test** (a no-op cycle produces a byte-identical file), verified verbatim at its cited location, `docs/closures/CR-00933-audit-record-nt-0012-0013-0014-adoption-docs-audit-checklists-work-item-close-md.md:114` — no mis-citation found, though one was looked for. The watcher's first draft read `watcher.md`'s "each cycle" claim as requiring `written_at` to move every cycle and filed this as a tool defect, which would have collided with RL-907(d) and forced a false choice; corrected here — "each cycle" describes the watcher unconditionally re-deriving its inputs and invoking `cycle`, not the timestamp `cycle` then writes. Behaviour satisfies the skill and the ruling; no field in the current schema (`written_by`/`written_at` are the only two per-block keys anywhere in the script or `SKILL.md`) records "checked and confirmed live" separately from "content last changed." **Same shape as F69** (the position block cannot express "no active slice" either) — different fields, same state-model-narrower-than-its-consumers shape; two instances, not one. **Amended 2026-09-02**: the gap is wider than the JSON. Reproduced directly — two identical `cycle` calls two seconds apart, against a canonical baseline, leave content byte-identical (RL-907(d) holds) **and leave the file's mtime unmoved too**, because `path.write_text`, the only statement that could advance it, sits inside the branch a genuine no-op never enters. **Nothing available on this file records liveness, not just `written_at`.** That matters because mtime is this repository's own established liveness idiom — `delivery-process.md:253` names "mtime staleness checks" as a watcher duty, and F67 records "`ps -p <pid>` and the state file's mtime are the adopted liveness proxies" directly — and for this one artifact, applying it gives a wrong answer silently. Full evidence: [`docs/findings/FD-01010-written-at-records-when-a-block-s-content-last-changed-not-when-the-writer-last-checked-it.md`](FD-01010-written-at-records-when-a-block-s-content-last-changed-not-when-the-writer-last-checked-it.md) | — | 2 | carry forward, unowned. **Amended 2026-09-02 — asks for a property, not a field.** The original proposal (a `checked_at` field inside the state file) is withdrawn and **ruled out, not merely revised, by RL-907(d) at its primary source** (`docs/rulings/RL-00907-q4-artifacts-win-where-an-artifact-exists-and-nothing-that-blocks-an-action-may-be-counted-in-b-without-one.md`, part (d)): a field refreshed every cycle inside the file is a freshness token moving while content is frozen, which the ruling names as F31's shape "regardless of what the surrounding prose says" — no field inside `runtime-state.json` can satisfy this row without violating that ruling. Asks instead for a no-op cycle to be observable **without changing the state file's bytes** — two candidate mechanisms named without ranking (`os.utime()` on every cycle; a separate sidecar timestamp file), the choice left to whoever builds it. Either must leave `tests/test_watcher_runtime_state.py::test_a_cycle_with_no_change_is_byte_identical` passing unmodified — confirmed that test asserts content bytes only, never mtime, so neither candidate conflicts with it. Not fix-before-close: F58 already carries the separate "nothing invokes this on a schedule" gap. Falsifiable: discharged by either mechanism landing with the cited test unmodified, or by a corrected reading showing the existing artifact already answers the liveness question. Full reasoning: [`docs/findings/FD-01010-written-at-records-when-a-block-s-content-last-changed-not-when-the-writer-last-checked-it.md`](FD-01010-written-at-records-when-a-block-s-content-last-changed-not-when-the-writer-last-checked-it.md) |
| The lead-staleness nudge ladder cannot distinguish a legitimate long wait from a dead lead, and its disambiguating signal was withdrawn for cause and never replaced (F73) | `.claude/skills/reporter-cycle/scripts/nudge.py`'s three-signal AND-conjunction (the `.last_lead_status_ts` marker, `eta.md`'s stamp, `origin/main`'s newest commit) — confirmed verbatim against the shipped script's own docstring — nudges only when every signal is stale, which correctly fixed the single-signal design's documented false positive but cannot tell a legitimate long wait from a dead lead: during any sufficiently long build all three go stale *together*, for the same reason, not independently. the actual bundled-data invariant is `backend/tests/test_lineage.py::test_no_reference_rows_are_bundled_in_the_repository`, a test, not a register finding, which fired correctly on W37-4's own fixture tonight and does not belong in this trio. **The corrected trio, same shape verified against each member**: F69 (`position.slice` cannot express "no active slice"), F72 (`written_at` cannot express "checked and unchanged"), and this row (the runtime-state ecosystem cannot express "work is in flight" generally — `in_flight_expensive_verifications` is scoped narrowly, per its own skill doc, to spec §8's announce/check protocol for one specific costly verification, not general work-dispatch state) — three instances of one shape in the same artifact family: the state model is narrower than its own consumers need. This supersedes the owner and event of the opening kept below: carry forward, unowned — **not** a case for reversing F31 (the withdrawal remains correct; a false signal removed and not replaced is still better than one kept) and **not** a threshold fix (the ladder has no better information available, which is the finding). Per the shared-shape framing: recommend extending artifact B's model with a general, re-derived-each-cycle "work in flight" signal — Ruling-47(d)-compliant by the same construction as `position` and `in_flight_expensive_verifications` already are — rather than patch `nudge.py` alone or rehabilitate `roster-state.md`; that signal, once built, is what the reporter's charter already anticipates reading. Sizing, field shape and ownership not decided here. Falsifiable: discharged by such a signal landing and the reporter's charter updated to read it, or by a corrected reading showing the existing artifacts already disambiguate. **Amended 2026-09-02 — two further states, neither discharged by the "work in flight" signal above.** (1) **Work complete, waiting on an external decision** — the state this amendment is itself written in: zero open PRs, `main` at `81e7c4e`, `eta.md`'s headline says the team stood down with only the maintainer's decision outstanding; `nudge.log`'s own last entry (`07:15:02Z`, all three signals stale) is a live instance. "Work in flight" reads correctly `false` here too, so it cannot be the disambiguating signal; this state needs a "named outstanding external dependency" signal instead. (2) **A relaying agent's published field is checked; its internal narrative is not** — the reporter's cycle posts `eta.md`'s headline verbatim by design and that field stayed correct; separately, in relaying nudges at `06:15:03Z` and `07:15:02Z`, it narrated "W37-4 still building" — first reported by the lead as recipient, **then confirmed by the reporter's own quoted text, supplied on request after this row's first push**: "W37-4 still building (no merges expected, origin/main stale as expected)" at 06:15, "Expected pattern continuing during W37-4 build" at 07:15 — still an author's account of its own output, not this review reading the channel, but independently corroborated by this review against `nudge.log` on two details neither party was asked for (the "third"/"fourth" nudge labelling and both marker-age figures, both matching to within a minute). Independently verified false by 2h09m and 3h09m respectively (`#573` merged `04:06:14Z`; twelve further PRs merged before the second relay, confirmed via `gh pr list`). A third instance, per the lead: the reporter's own hedge narrowing this to "accurate through 04:15" is itself independently verified false, by nine minutes. The mechanism: `report-spec.md`'s re-derivation discipline covers structured facts (open PRs, merges), never the free-text narrative around them — nothing checks the belief the way something checks the field, and the checked field is the one that stayed right. | 63 in-scope files cannot carry a header at all, and their custody is an exemption rather than a sidecar (F83) | **Measured, not asserted**: 59 `.json` + 1 `.yaml` under `docs/contracts/` cannot hold YAML front matter — JSON has no comment syntax, so `---` … `---` makes the file invalid and `json.load`, the OpenAPI toolchain and the frontend's generated client all fail at parse — plus the 3 unparseable vendored `SKILL.md` manifests, which `CLAUDE.md` §12 forbids editing. The single `.md` under `docs/contracts/` **can** hold one and is deliberately not exempt: the set is scoped to files that physically cannot, not to a directory. The maintainer's gap-2 ruling had specified a generator-emitted header with the fallback *"if that's a bigger change than a `generated: true` exemption in check 35, say so and I'll take the exemption"* — it is not bigger, it is **impossible for 60 of the 63**, and the fallback was taken on that basis. Full analysis, the sidecar design that was rejected, and both enforcement conditions: [`FD-01020-63-in-scope-files-cannot-carry-a-header-and-their-custody-is-an-exemption-rather-than-a-sidecar.md`](FD-01020-63-in-scope-files-cannot-carry-a-header-and-their-custody-is-an-exemption-rather-than-a-sidecar.md) | W37-5c | 2 | **accept, with instrument** — the maintainer's ruling, 2026-09-02. Exemption, **not** a sidecar; the sidecar is recorded as the option not taken so the design is recoverable if the 63 ever need machine-readable ownership. Two conditions, both enforceable and both owed by W37-5c: **(1)** every exempt entry cites its reason and the ruling permitting it — a list whose entries carry no justification is indistinguishable from a list of things nobody got round to; **(2)** the exempt set is itself checked, the count of unstamped in-scope files equalling the exempt list, **so the list cannot grow silently**. Condition 2 is RL-985's property applied to an exemption rather than a census and inherits its reasoning: the check **names** the unstamped-and-not-exempt files, never compares two totals, because two errors that cancel pass a total-only check. Filed as a finding rather than left as an allowlist so the 63 have custody under `RFC-778` — a deferred item with no owner is not deferred, it is lost. **Corrected 2026-09-02, after the ruling: the population is 65, not 63.** This row's claim and evidence cells both state 63 and are superseded here rather than edited, per this file's annotated-in-place rule. Two tracked files in the ruled stamp set were never counted, both meeting the finding's own criterion that a format with no comment syntax cannot carry front matter: `docs/process/delivery-process.core.json` (CLAUDE.md §15's machine-readable extract, where front matter breaks `json.load` for the reason the 59 contracts schemas do) and `docs/research/file-census-5ef559d.csv`. So 60 plus 2 non-`.md` files, plus the 3 unparseable vendored manifests, is 65; measured at `7186dca` and stable across `e63332c` and `544b90c`, since #629 touched no file under `docs/`. **The counting predicate is stated once, in F83's own dated correction section, and deliberately not restated here** — it contains a shell pipe that would split this row, and `CLAUDE.md` §13 refuses a pasted constant for the same reason `RFC-756` refuses a duplicated status line: the copy is what goes stale. **The defect was the corpus, not the arithmetic** — the lead measured the two populations already in mind and reported their union as the unstampable files, never enumerating the stamp set the rule ranges over, which is the class `F85` describes, committed by `F85`'s own author four hours after §13 was amended to require the predicate a count was counted with. The two new entries ship flagged in their own `ruling` cell as found by this check and awaiting ratification, reversible by deleting two tuples. **Condition 2 found them**, on its first day against the real corpus, which is the property it was imposed for. Full correction with the predicate: [`FD-01020-63-in-scope-files-cannot-carry-a-header-and-their-custody-is-an-exemption-rather-than-a-sidecar.md`](FD-01020-63-in-scope-files-cannot-carry-a-header-and-their-custody-is-an-exemption-rather-than-a-sidecar.md), section *Dated correction — 2026-09-02*. |

| A session ends leaving the shared checkout in a state its successor cannot fast-forward, and nothing announces it (F97) | **Measured at `e97b97a` before remediation, predicates verbatim.** Two residues in the shared checkout at the start of the next session. (1) A stale lock: `ls -la .git/index.lock` → **0 bytes, `Sep 2 22:16`**, `pgrep -af git` → **no process holding it**; the checkout's own `HEAD` (`32fc63c`) carries committer date `2026-09-02T22:21:08+01:00`, **five minutes later**, so the lock is residue of a different, abandoned operation. (2) A stale `main`: `git rev-list --count 32fc63c..e97b97a` → **28**, `git rev-list --count e97b97a..32fc63c` → **0** — no divergence, a clean fast-forward simply not taken. Composed, they make `git merge --ff-only origin/main` fail with a message naming an open editor and a crashed process, **neither of which was the case**; the correct action is reached only by disbelieving the message enough to check. `.claude/roles/lead.md:66-70` binds every *member* into its own worktree and **says nothing about the state the shared checkout is left in**, and the halt protocol's worktree clause (`docs/plans/PL-01037-w37-6-the-extended-window-s-second-fail-and-the-handover-2026-09-03.md` §10.1) verifies each member worktree is both clean and pushed but records the shared checkout as *"clean; not removed"* — **`git status` reports neither a stale lock nor a stale `main`**, so the check that was run could not have found either. Same two-cause shape as RFC-842's outage (`docs/rfcs/RFC-00842-a-credential-in-an-ephemeral-job-directory-is-borrowed-not-stored-and-is-found-by-its-shape-not-its-container-s-name.md:16-19`): state left where it stops being usable, and the diagnostic that looks for it afterward checks the wrong thing. **Scope stated honestly: one occurrence measured, the class not** — prior boundaries would be evidenced only in session-local state that no longer exists. (2) `_check_unstampable_register` (check 35's F83 condition 2 clause, `scripts/audit-docs.py:2706`) calls `nt0019_stamp_set()` with no argument, which asks `git ls-files` for the **whole real repository** — never gated by `ROOT`, `_ID_SCOPE_ROOTS` or `migrated_tree()` at all — polluting 8 "reds alone" tests (`test_check_30_reds_alone_on_an_unknown_field`, `test_check_30_reds_alone_on_a_ledger_prs_field`, `test_check_30_positive_control_a_clean_finding_essay_passes`, `test_check_31_reds_alone_on_a_header_filename_mismatch`, `test_check_33_reds_alone_on_a_status_outside_the_vocabulary`, `test_check_33_reds_alone_on_asymmetric_supersedes`, `test_check_34_reds_alone_on_a_dangling_corrected_by_entry`, `test_check_37_reds_alone_on_a_missing_required_section`) that share `_run_all_ten` with an extra failure from an unrelated real file, on a migrated tree specifically: `docs/REDIRECTS.csv` is not in RFC-937's stamp set nor the F83 exemption register — `check 35: docs/REDIRECTS.csv: in RFC-937's stamp set and cannot carry a header (...), but is not in the F83 exemption register — add it with its reason and the ruling that permits it, or make the file stampable` and `check 35: docs/REDIRECTS.csv: in the checks-30-39 scope with no parseable header, and not in the F83 exemption register — stamp it, or register it with its reason and the ruling that permits it` (both lines reproduced verbatim from a real `audit-docs.py` run inside that tree — the gap stays caught by the unpinned, full-suite run; only these 11 tests' own hermeticity was the defect). Production `audit-docs.py` is not wrong: it always runs inside the tree it audits, so gating on the real `ROOT`/`git ls-files` there is correct. The tests' own fixtures silently assumed a pre-migration real tree instead of pinning it. | W37-6 | 2 | **fixed before close, this same PR (D2).** `tests/test_audit_docs_ids.py`'s `_run_all_ten` helper (and the three direct gate tests) now pins **four** attributes, quoted at their own lines: `monkeypatch.setattr(audit, "ROOT", tmp_path)` (`tests/test_audit_docs_ids.py:130`, an empty, per-test tmp dir, guaranteed to carry neither `INDEX.md` nor `REDIRECTS.csv`), `monkeypatch.setattr(audit, "nt0019_stamp_set", lambda tracked=None: [])` (`:131`), `monkeypatch.setattr(audit, "UNSTAMPABLE_EXEMPTIONS", ())` (`:132`), and `monkeypatch.setattr(audit, "migrated_tree", lambda: False)` (`:133`, the fourth pin — not previously named in this row — that gates `check_citations`/`check_redirects`'s own `ROOT`-relative reads and every other `migrated_tree()` caller back to the pre-migration branch the fixture's empty `tmp_path` alone does not force). After the third S3 stop the executor relaunched its gate detached with `setsid`, reading the kill as the harness's. The fix is #884's charter lines (background with a cancel file or a foreground `timeout`; never relaunch a killed process detached; a full gate only after the lead's slot grant under the flock), with the lead-held slot flock as the mechanical control. Full evidence: [`FD-01214-a-foreground-blocked-executor-cannot-receive-a-lead-stop-and-one-relaunched-its-gate-detached.md`](FD-01214-a-foreground-blocked-executor-cannot-receive-a-lead-stop-and-one-relaunched-its-gate-detached.md) | WK-1178 | 2 | **deferred with an owner — WK-1178**, 2026-09-28 (the deputy's entries of 22:18:07 and 23:30:16 BST). #884 (`633c6f34`) delivered S-11 (foreground with a `timeout`), S-12 (never relaunch a killed process detached), S-13 (a full gate only on the lead's grant, under the slot flock) and S-14. **Two residues:** S-11 bounds a foreground call and does not make it receivable, so the lead's slot flock stays the control; and S-13 says a named single test needs no grant (`executor.md:109`–`:113`), so a targeted run needs no grant as written. The first quiet-window breach after the charter merged was executor-s1's **four** targeted pytest runs in `trees/executor-sanitiser` inside the lead's exclusive T7-2 window of 23:26 BST on 2026-09-28 (about 23:26:30 to 23:31:33, per executor-s1's account as the lead relayed it, reconstructed to within about 30 s; the last, PID 2301135, `949 passed`; commit `60f95f02` at 22:29:36Z, pushed at 22:31:43Z, read from git and GitHub). executor-s1 did not see the 23:26 notice during any of the four, because messages arrive only between an agent's turns, so the run broke the lead's window instruction and not S-13 as written; the lead let it finish because of S-14's residue risk. Fix, per the deputy's entry of 23:33:55 BST: the lead writes a hold file (token and head); a root `conftest.py` `pytest_configure` hook refuses to run while it exists unless `GIP_GATE_TOKEN` matches, naming the holder; three tests (refuses without the token, runs with it, runs normally with no file) plus a positive control that the gate runner passes the token; S-13 amended to cover any pytest in a window and the frontend runner, or to state why not. Built by a WK-1178 executor after S3 merges, in the FD-1218 PR. Event: that PR merges and the auditor reads the hook, its tests and the amended S-13 in `executor.md`. **Second incident, 2026-09-29 (maintainer's entry (given on the maintainer's behalf) of 09:48:51 BST):** executor-m1 ran a full gate (PIDs 24822 and 26277, `trees/executor-m1c`, load 4.91) with no slot granted, inside S3's T7-3 window; the entry calls it a breach of S-13 and says the hook is still unbuilt. A stop is asked for in the entry and is not read here. **Third instance, 2026-09-29 (the maintainer's entry of 11:13:36 BST):** executor-s3fix started a gate without its merge-in and did not answer a STOP while blocked in the foreground; the maintainer accepted the running gate at `3a3e0277` as #886's full gate. The entry of 11:27:38 BST names PID 296584 (#886) as running at 10:27 UTC. No start time or STOP time is stated here. **As stated by the executor (executor-s3fix's own account, relayed by the lead; not read from a log here):** it launched #886's gate at 10:09:01Z on the lead's first grant, after the prior pytest (PID 224799) had exited at 10:08:29Z, and not from a wait loop; it ran the gate as a background job under `timeout 3600` and polled with `kill -0`, which deviates from S-11; and none of the lead's messages, including the 10:11:15Z STOP, reached it until the gate ended at 10:33:49Z.  **#891 closed unmerged, 2026-09-29:** the maintainer's entry "2026-09-29 11:35:28 BST · maintainer (acting on the maintainer's behalf) · #891: CLOSE unmerged; rework as a new WK-1178 PR" (`to-lead.md`) closes #891 and supersedes it with a new WK-1178 PR built to this row's Disposition. **The fix this Disposition specifies is now owned by that rework PR.** This finding stays open. **Fourth instance, 2026-09-29:** the maintainer's entry of 12:19:51 BST ("2026-09-29 12:19:51 BST · maintainer (acting on the maintainer's behalf) · LOAD incident: the #876 repro loops stopped by me", `to-lead.md`) records that at 11:19:28 UTC the 1-minute load was 20.15 and that four orphaned repro loops (bash PIDs 543605, 543606, 543608 and 543610, ppid 1) were each respawning `.venv/bin/python …/tmp/oldchild.py` at 135–240% CPU. They were owned by no live agent command. **They were stopped by the maintainer's kill of the four orphaned repro loops**, by PID and then their python children, at about 11:19:30 UTC; by 11:19:41 UTC none remained. The entry names it "the FD-1214 detached pattern again": a repro started by an agent that outlives the agent's command. The entry does not state how the loops were launched. **As stated by auditor-a-2 (its own account, relayed by the lead; not read from a log here):** the killed loops were its batch 3: `for k in 1 2 3 4; do ( for i in $(seq 1 30); do .venv/bin/python …/oldchild.py …; … done; echo done$k >> …/ofail.log ) & done; wait 2>/dev/null \| true; echo launched`. The `wait` was the last element of a pipeline, so it ran in a subshell and waited for nothing; the tool call returned at once, and the four `( … ) &` subshells were reparented to PID 1 when the shell exited. There was no trap, no PID file and no exit confirmation, and no `done<k>` marker was ever written, so batch 3 never finished. Batch 3 started at about 11:18:10 UTC, when batches 1 and 2 had completed, and its four subshells were still running their 30-run loops when they were killed at about 11:19:30 UTC; there was no respawning beyond the loops themselves. Batches 1 and 2 (completed earlier, 11:12:28–11:18:10 UTC) were `setsid nohup …/loop.sh <w> 40 >/dev/null 2>&1 &` four times: detached by design, no `nice`, no load or gate check. A later `pkill -f oldchild.py -u $USER` matched its own command line and killed its own call (exit 144), so it did not stop them. It withdrew all its reproduction counts as evidence. | The premium ladder attributes a clamp's effect to the office_premium rung, not to the constraints rung (FD-1330) | **Severity medium** (a transparency mislabel on a governed artifact). `FR-247` (`03:154`) puts "± constraints (min premium, capping)" on the `constraints` rung. | FR-275's intermediate precision limb is not met at runtime, because the ZEN engine silently rounds its own intermediates at 28–29 significant digits (FD-1331) | **Severity low.** FR-275 (`03-rating-engine.md:224`) says bundle compilation checks that no rate table value, constant, or intermediate needs a decimal scale beyond 28 and fails with a named error rather than a silent loss of precision. `_check_scale_cap` (`compile.py:199-222`) covers literals and input bounds, never an intermediate, which is a run-time value. Measured at `25ca36df` on `zen-engine` 0.53.0: a product with exactly 30 significant digits (`…835.1851851835`) comes back as `…835.185185184` (29 digits), and a 47-digit product comes back at 29, with no error; the effect is about 1e-28 relative. ## F42 is retired, recorded 2026-08-30

**F42 names no finding and never will.** It was allocated for the `req-coverage.py`
mislabel, then withdrawn before it was ever filed here: **F36** already registered that
defect, in nearly the same words. The number is a tombstone, not a gap — a reader who finds
F41 followed by F43 has found this note, not an omission.

**Why it is tombstoned rather than re-used.** After the withdrawal the number was briefly
re-allocated to a second, unrelated finding — the concurrent-`pytest` truncation that is
`F40`'s sibling, where two runs collide with no interruption involved. That finding is
**F45**. Re-using a withdrawn number left one artifact carrying `F42` in two incompatible
senses at once, so a citation to it could not be resolved without knowing the allocation
history. `CLAUDE.md` §5 forbids exactly this for requirement ids and section numbers, and
the register's finding ids are held to the same rule: **never renumber, never re-use;
append, mark superseded, or leave a tombstone.**

The renumber cost one file and one task. The precedent — a register that breaks §5 while
the phase review argues from §5 — would have cost more, which is the ground the decision
was taken on.
